=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There exists an undirected tree with n nodes numbered 0 to n - 1. You are given a 2D integer array edges of length n - 1, where edges[i] = [u_i, v_i, w_i] indicates that there is an edge between nodes u_i and v_i with weight w_i in the tree.
Your task is to remove zero or more edges such that:

Each node has an edge with at most k other nodes, where k is given.
The sum of the weights of the remaining edges is maximized.

Return the maximum possible sum of weights for the remaining edges after making the necessary removals.
 
Example 1:

Input: edges = [[0,1,4],[0,2,2],[2,3,12],[2,4,6]], k = 2
Output: 22
Explanation:


Node 2 has edges with 3 other nodes. We remove the edge [0, 2, 2], ensuring that no node has edges with more than k = 2 nodes.
The sum of weights is 22, and we can't achieve a greater sum. Thus, the answer is 22.


Example 2:

Input: edges = [[0,1,5],[1,2,10],[0,3,15],[3,4,20],[3,5,5],[0,6,10]], k = 3
Output: 65
Explanation:

Since no node has edges connecting it to more than k = 3 nodes, we don't remove any edges.
The sum of weights is 65. Thus, the answer is 65.


 
Constraints:

2 <= n <= 10^5
1 <= k <= n - 1
edges.length == n - 1
edges[i].length == 3
0 <= edges[i][0] <= n - 1
0 <= edges[i][1] <= n - 1
1 <= edges[i][2] <= 10^6
The input is generated such that edges form a valid tree.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def maximizeSumOfWeights(self, edges: List[List[int]], k: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

    for v,w in adj[u]:
        if parent[v] == u: # child
            sum_a += dp0[v]
            gain = w + dp1[v] - dp0[v]
            if gain > 0:
                gains.append(gain)
    gains.sort(reverse=True)
    m = len(gains)
    # compute prefix sum maybe
    pref = 0
    limit0 = min(k, m)
    for i in range(limit0):
        pref += gains[i]
    dp0[u] = sum_a + pref
    limit1 = min(k - 1, m)
    pref1 = 0
    for i in range(limit1):
        pref1 += gains[i]
    dp1[u] = sum_a + pref1

Now, final code:
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximizeSumOfWeights(self, edges: List[List[int]], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(edges) + 1
        adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for u, v, w in edges:
            adj[u].append((v, w))
            adj[v].append((u, w))
        
        parent = [-2] * n
        parent[0] = -1
        order = []
        stack = [0]
        while stack:
            u = stack.pop()
            order.append(u)
            for v, w in adj[u]:
                if v == parent[u]:
                    continue
                if parent[v] == -2:
                    parent[v] = u
                    stack.append(v)
        
        dp0 = [0] * n
        dp1 = [0] * n
        
        for u in reversed(order):
            base = 0
            gains = []
            for v, w in adj[u]:
                if parent[v] == u:
                    base += dp0[v]
                    gain = w + dp1[v] - dp0[v]
                    if gain > 0:
                        gains.append(gain)
            gains.sort(reverse=True)
            pref = [0]
            for g in gains:
                pref.append(pref[-1] + g)
            m = len(gains)
            dp0[u] = base + pref[min(k, m)]
            dp1[u] = base + pref[min(k - 1, m)]
        
        return dp0[0]
```

Could avoid prefix list to reduce memory by computing top sums with two loops:
```
s = 0
for i in range(min(k,m)): s += gains[i]
dp0 = base + s
s = 0
for i in range(min(k-1,m)): s += gains[i]
dp1 = base + s
```
This avoids prefix list and extra ints. It loops over top k twice; if k large, O(m) twice, still O(m). Sorting dominates. This might be more memory efficient and equally simple. Let's consider using this instead of prefix list. Code:
```
gains.sort(reverse=True)
```

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximizeSumOfWeights(self, edges: List[List[int]], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(edges) + 1
        adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for u, v, w in edges:
            adj[u].append((v, w))
            adj[v].append((u, w))

        parent = [-2] * n
        parent[0] = -1
        order = []
        stack = [0]

        while stack:
            u = stack.pop()
            order.append(u)
            for v, _ in adj[u]:
                if v == parent[u]:
                    continue
                if parent[v] == -2:
                    parent[v] = u
                    stack.append(v)

        dp0 = [0] * n  # edge to parent is NOT kept
        dp1 = [0] * n  # edge to parent IS kept

        for u in reversed(order):
            base = 0
            gains = []

            for v, w in adj[u]:
                if parent[v] == u:
                    base += dp0[v]
                    gain = w + dp1[v] - dp0[v]
                    if gain > 0:
                        gains.append(gain)

            gains.sort(reverse=True)

            pref = [0]
            for g in gains:
                pref.append(pref[-1] + g)

            m = len(gains)
            dp0[u] = base + pref[min(k, m)]
            dp1[u] = base + pref[min(max(0, k - 1), m)]

        return dp0[0]
```
